\iota^{\pm}:({\mathbb{D}}^{2},h_{\pm})\rightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
. We denote by
\Omega_{n}
(resp.
\Omega
) the convex domain bounded by
\iota^{\pm}_{n}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})
(resp.
\iota^{\pm}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})
), and denote by
I^{\pm}_{n}
(resp.
I^{\pm}
) the induced metric on
\partial_{\pm}\Omega_{n}
(resp.
\partial_{\pm}\Omega
), which is isometric to
h^{\pm}_{n}
(resp.
h_{\pm}
).

We claim that
\partial_{\infty}\Omega
is a quasi-circle in
{\mathbb{C}P}^{1}
(which implies that
\Omega
is a convex domain of second type). To see this, since the principal curvatures of
\partial_{\pm}\Omega_{n}=\iota^{\pm}_{n}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})
are uniformly bounded between two positive constants (as shown above). This implies that the hyperbolic Gauss map say
G^{\pm}_{n}
from
\iota^{\pm}_{n}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})
to
D^{\pm}_{n}
(where
D^{+}_{n}\cup D^{+}_{n}={\mathbb{C}P}^{1}\setminus\partial_{\infty}\Omega_{n}
) is uniformly quasi-conformal (note that
G^{\pm}_{n}
is a homeomorphism and its boundary map
\partial G^{\pm}_{n}
is the identity from
\partial_{\infty}\iota^{\pm}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})=\partial_{\infty}\Omega_{n}
to itself). Recall that the
I
-gluing map of
\Omega_{n}
is
\Phi^{I}_{\Omega_{n}}=(\partial\varphi^{I_{-}}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{I_{+}}_{\Omega_{n}}
, where
\varphi^{I_{\pm}}_{\Omega_{n}}:{\mathbb{H}}^{2\pm}\rightarrow\partial_{\pm}\Omega_{n}
is a map such that
(\varphi^{I_{\pm}}_{\Omega_{n}})^{*}(I^{\pm}_{n})
is conformal to
h^{\pm}_{0}
. Let
\varphi^{c\pm}_{\Omega_{n}}:{\mathbb{H}}^{2\pm}\rightarrow D^{\pm}_{n}
be a bi-holomorphic map such that
(\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}}
is the conformal welding of the quasi-circle
\partial_{\infty}\Omega_{n}
. In particular, we have

\begin{split}f_{n}=\Phi^{I}_{\Omega_{n}}&=(\partial\varphi^{I-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}}\circ\big((\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\circ\big((\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{I+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\\
&=\big((\partial\varphi^{I-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial(G_{n}^{-})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\circ\big((\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\circ\big((\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial G^{+}_{n}\circ\partial\varphi^{I+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\\
&=\partial\big(\big((\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ G^{-}_{n}\circ\varphi^{I-}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)^{-1}\big)\circ\big((\partial\varphi^{c-}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\circ\partial\big((\varphi^{c+}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ G^{+}_{n}\circ\varphi^{I+}_{\Omega_{n}}\big)\end{split}

By the above discussion,
(\varphi^{c\pm}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ G^{\pm}_{n}\circ(\varphi^{I\pm}_{\Omega_{n}})
is a uniformly quasiconformal map (see e.g.
[
10
, Proposition 3.14]
) and its boundary map is thus a uniformly quasisymmetric homeomorphism. It then follows from hypothesis (ii) that the conformal welding of
\partial_{\infty}\Omega_{n}
is uniformly quasisymmetric, so by Theorem
5.3
, the
\partial_{\infty}\iota_{n}^{\pm}({\mathbb{D}}^{2})=\partial_{\infty}\Omega_{n}
are quasi-circles with a quasi-conformal constant which is bounded from above by a fixed constant.

Since
\partial_{\infty}(\partial_{\pm}\Omega)=\partial_{\infty}\Omega
has more than one point, by assumption (iv), Lemma
5.4
and Carathéodory’s theorem,
(\varphi^{I_{\pm}}_{\Omega_{n}})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
has a subsequence
(\varphi^{I_{\pm}}_{\Omega_{n_{k}}})_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
converging to a map, say
\varphi^{\pm}_{\Omega}:{\mathbb{H}}^{2\pm}\rightarrow\partial_{\pm}\Omega
, with
(\varphi^{\pm}_{\Omega})^{*}I_{\pm}
conformal to
h^{\pm}_{0}
. Since the gluing map
\Phi^{I}_{\Omega_{n}}=f_{n}=(\partial\varphi^{I_{-}}_{\Omega_{n}})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{I_{+}}_{\Omega_{n}}
is normalized, the map
(\partial\varphi^{I_{-}}_{\Omega})^{-1}\circ\partial\varphi^{I_{+}}_{\Omega}
, as the limit of
f_{n_{k}}
, is also normalized and is thus the
I
-gluing map
\Phi^{I}_{\Omega}
of
\Omega
(combined with the above properties about
\varphi^{I_{\pm}}_{\Omega}
). By assumption (iii),
f_{n}=\Phi^{I}_{\Omega_{n}}
converges to
f
. As a consequence,
\Phi^{I}_{\Omega}=f
. The lemma follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Let
D_{-},D_{+}\subset{\mathbb{C}P}^{1}
be disjoint quasi-disks with
\partial D_{-}=\partial D_{+}
, denoted by
C
for simplicity, which is oriented such that
D_{+}
and
D_{-}
lie on the left-hand and right-hand sides of
C
. Using Theorem
5.3
, the conformal welding
\Phi_{C}
is quasisymmetric. By Proposition
5.9
,
\Phi_{C}
can be approximated by a sequence of uniformly quasisymmetric quasifuchsian homeomorphisms say
f_{n}
. We assume that
f_{n}
is
(\rho^{+}_{n},\rho^{-}_{n})
-equivariant, with
\rho^{+}_{n}
and
\rho^{-}_{n}
two Fuchsian representations of the same surface group
\Gamma_{n}
.

Note that since
\Gamma_{n}
is a surface group,
\Gamma_{n}
is residually finite – this follows from Selberg’s Lemma, or from
[
21
]
.
Therefore, there exists a finite-index subgroup of
\Gamma_{n}
(which does not contain enough finitely many non-trivial elements of
\Gamma_{n}
) such that its representation under
\rho^{\pm}_{n}
has a fundamental domain
P^{\pm}_{n}
in
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
containing a closed hyperbolic disk
B_{H}(0,\tau_{n})
, where
\tau_{n}
strictly increases to
\infty
.

For the given metric
h_{\pm}
on
D_{\pm}
, we consider the conformal metric
(\varphi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}h_{\pm}
on
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
, where
\varphi_{0}^{\pm}:({\mathbb{D}}^{2},h_{0})\rightarrow(D_{\pm},h_{\pm})
is a conformal map. Applying Proposition
5.12
for the conformal metric
(\varphi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}h_{\pm}
and the sequence
(\rho^{\pm}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
of Fuchsian representations, there is a sequence of
\rho^{\pm}_{n}
-invariant conformal metrics, say
h^{\pm}_{n}
, on
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
, which uniformly converges to
(\varphi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}h_{\pm}
smoothly on compact subsets of
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
.

For the given metric
h^{*}_{\pm}
on
D_{\pm}
, we consider the conformal metric
(\psi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}h^{*}_{\pm}
on
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
, where
\psi^{\pm}_{0}:({\mathbb{D}}^{2},h_{0})\rightarrow(D_{\pm},h^{*}_{\pm})
is a conformal map. Applying Proposition
5.11
for the conformal metric
(\psi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}(h^{*}_{\pm})
and the sequence
(\rho^{\pm}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
of Fuchsian representations, there is a sequence of
\rho^{\pm}_{n}
-invariant conformal metrics, say
h^{*\pm}_{n}
, on
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
, which uniformly converges to
(\psi^{\pm}_{0})^{*}(h^{*}_{\pm})
smoothly on compact subsets of
{\mathbb{D}}^{2}
.

We divide the discussion into two cases below, according to the types of convex domains we want to realize.

Case 1
: For Statements (i)-(ii), it suffices to show Statements (i), since Statements (ii) will follow in a similar way, by replacing
h_{-}
with
h^{*}_{-}
.

Applying Statement (1) of Proposition
5.14
for the
(\rho^{+}_{n},\rho^{-}_{n})
-equivariant quasisymmetric homeomorphism
f_{n}
and
\rho^{-}_{n}
-invariant metric
h^{-}_{n}
, there exists a convex domain
\Omega_{n}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
of first type, invariant under a quasifuchsian group, with the induced metric on
\partial\Omega_{n}
isometric to
h^{-}_{n}
and the
I
-gluing map of
\Omega_{n}
being
f_{n}
. Up to isometries, we can assume that
\Omega_{n}
are located in good positions (namely, their boundary are realized by isometric immersions of
({\mathbb{D}}^{2},h^{-}_{n})
with the images of 0 contained in a compact subset of
{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
). By Proposition
5.16
, after passing to a subsequence,
(\Omega_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
converges to a convex domain
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
of first type with the induced metric on
\partial\Omega
isometric to
h_{-}
, and the
I
-gluing map of
{\Omega}
being
\Phi_{C}
. Combined with Proposition
5.13
, Statement (i) follows.

Case 2
: For Statements (iii)-(v), we just need to show one of them, since the others will follow in the same manner, by replacing
h_{\pm}
with
h^{*}_{\pm}
. We show Statement (iii) for instance.

Applying Statement (3) of Proposition
5.14
for the
(\rho^{+}_{n},\rho^{-}_{n})
-equivariant quasisymmetric homeomorphism
f_{n}
and
\rho^{\pm}_{n}
-invariant metric
h^{\pm}_{n}
, there exists a convex domain
\Omega_{n}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
of second type, which is invariant under a quasifuchsian group, with the induced metric on
\partial_{\pm}\Omega_{n}
isometric to
h^{\pm}_{n}
and the
I
-gluing map of
\Omega_{n}
being
f_{n}
. Up to isometries, we can assume that
\Omega_{n}
are located in good positions (that is, their boundary components are realized by isometric immersions of
({\mathbb{D}}^{2},h^{\pm}_{n})
with the images of 0 contained in compact subsets of
{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
). By Proposition
5.20
, after passing to a subsequence,
(\Omega_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}
converges to a convex domain
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{3}
of second type with the induced metric on
\partial_{\pm}\Omega
is isometric to
h_{\pm}
, and the
I
-gluing map of
{\Omega}
being
\Phi_{C}
. Combined with Proposition
5.13
, Statement (iii) follows.
∎

6.
Applications

6.1.
New parametrizations of Bers slice

Recall that a Bers slice is the space of quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifolds with a fixed conformal structure at one prescribed boundary at infinity (up to isotopy). We denote by
{{\mathcal{B}}}_{c}
the Bers slice with a fixed conformal structure
c
at the prescribed boundary at infinity. By the Bers’ simultaneous uniformization theorem,
{{\mathcal{B}}}_{c}
is parameterized by
{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
in terms of the conformal structure on the other boundary at infinity. In addition,
{{\mathcal{B}}}_{c}
is uniquely determined by the bending lamination (resp. the induced metric) on the boundary component of the convex core facing the prescribed boundary at infinity with conformal structure
c
, by the Grafting parametrization theorem given by Scannell-Wolf
[
36
]
(resp. Dumas-Wolf
[
17
]
).

As an application of the special cases of Corollary
1.3
and Corollary
1.4
when the prescribed metrics
h
and
h^{*}
have constant curvature, we obtain a new parametrization of
{\mathcal{B}}_{c}
in terms of constant curvature surfaces. More precisely, let
K\in(-1,0)
, we define
\Phi_{K}:{\mathcal{B}}_{c}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}(S)
as the map that sends
M\in{\mathcal{B}}_{c}
to the hyperbolic metric homothetic to the induced metric on the
K
-surface in
M
on the opposite side of the prescribed boundary at infinity.
Let
K^{*}\in(-\infty,0)
, we define
\Psi_{K^{*}}:{\mathcal{B}}_{c}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}(S)
as the map that sends
M\in{\mathcal{B}}_{c}
to the hyperbolic metric homothetic to the third fundamental form on the
K^{*}/(1-K^{*})
-surface in
M
on the opposite side of the prescribed boundary at infinity. Then we have the following statement.

Corollary 6.1
.

For each
K\in(-1,0)
and
K^{*}\in(-\infty,0)
, the maps
\Phi_{K}
and
\Psi_{K^{*}}
are both homeomorphisms.

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.5

Another application of Corollary
1.3
is Theorem
1.5
. We recall it here for convenience. Let
h
be a hyperbolic metric on
S
, and let
c\in{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
be a conformal structure on
S
. We want to show that there is a quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifold with the conformal structure on its upper boundary component at infinity isotopic to
c
, and with the induced metric on the lower boundary component of its convex core isotopic to
h
. To see this, we first show the following compactness statement:

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
K_{n}\in(-1,0)
with
K_{n}
decreasingly converging to
-1
and let
h_{n}=(1/|K_{n}|)h
. Let
M_{n}
be a sequence of quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifolds with the conformal structure on its upper boundary component at infinity isotopic to
c
, and with the induced metric on the
K_{n}
-surface in the lower side of its convex core isotopic to
h_{n}
. Then up to extracting a subsequence,
M_{n}
converges to a quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifold with the conformal structure on its upper boundary component at infinity isotopic to
c
, and with the induced metric on the lower boundary component of its convex core isotopic to
h
.

Proof.

Let
\Sigma_{n}
be the
K_{n}
-surface in the lower connected component of the complement of the convex core in
M_{n}
. We denote by
\partial_{-}C_{M_{n}}
the lower boundary of the convex core, and by
m_{n}^{-}
its induced metric.

Note that the nearest-point projection from
\Sigma_{n}
to
\partial_{-}C_{M_{n}}
is contracting. As a consequence,
m_{n}^{-}\leq h_{n}=(1/|K_{n}|)h
(in the sense of comparison of the length spectrum) and since
K_{n}\to-1
, it follows that
m^{-}_{n}\to h
(by the positive definiteness of Thurston’s asymmetric metric on
{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
, see
[
46
, Prop. 2.1]
).

Since
c_{n}^{-}
(the conformal structure at infinity on the lower boundary component) is uniformly quasi-conformal to
m_{n}^{-}
(see e.g.
[
19
]
), it follows that
(c_{n}^{-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
remains in a compact subset of
{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
. After extracting a sub-sequence, it converges to a limit
c^{-}
, and
(M_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
converges to the quasifuchsian manifold
M
having conformal structures
c
and
c^{-}
on the upper and lower ideal boundary components, and having induced metric
h
on the lower boundary component of the convex core.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Let
K_{n}\in(-1,0)
with
K_{n}
decreasingly converging to
-1
and let
h_{n}=(1/|K_{n}|)h
. By Corollary
1.3
, there is a unique (up to isometries) quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifold, denoted by
M_{n}
, with the conformal structure on its upper boundary component at infinity isotopic to
c
, and with the induced metric on the
K_{n}
-surface in the lower side of its convex core isotopic to
h_{n}
. It then follows from Proposition
6.2
that the sequence
(M_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
converges to a quasifuchsian hyperbolic manifold with the conformal structure on its upper boundary component at infinity isotopic to
c
, and with the induced metric on the lower boundary component of its convex core isotopic to
h
, which is a desired manifold. This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.5
.
∎
